```
use super::*;

/// A fresh folder of its own under the system's temporary one, removed when dropped.
struct Folder(PathBuf);

impl Folder {
    fn new() -> Self {
        let mut nonce = [0u8; 8];
        getrandom::fill(&mut nonce).unwrap();
        let path = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("opener-{}", u64::from_ne_bytes(nonce)));
        std::fs::create_dir(&path).unwrap();
        Self(std::fs::canonicalize(path).unwrap())
    }

    fn file(&self, name: &str) -> PathBuf {
        let path = self.0.join(name);
        std::fs::write(&path, "x").unwrap();
        path
    }
}

impl Drop for Folder {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);
    }
}

fn calls(desktop: Desktop) -> Vec<Call> {
    let Desktop::Recorded(calls) = desktop else {
        unreachable!("the tests' desktop records")
    };
    calls
}

#[test]
fn documents_may_launch_and_anything_that_could_run_may_not() {
    for allowed in [
        "/r/notes.txt",
        "/r/data.JSON",
        "/r/photo.png",
        "/r/report.pdf",
        "/r/main.rs",
        "/r/archive.tar.txt",
    ] {
        assert!(may_launch(Path::new(allowed)), "{allowed} was refused");
    }
    for refused in [
        "/r/run.sh",
        "/r/tool.py",
        "/r/app.js",
        "/r/page.html",
        "/r/image.svg",
        "/r/page.xml",
        "/r/launcher.desktop",
        "/r/setup.exe",
        "/r/script.ps1",
        "/r/Makefile",
        "/r/LICENSE",
        "/r/.txt",
        "/r/notes.txt.sh",
        // A direction override paints this as «xexe.txt»; a line break would run on paste.
        "/r/x\u{202e}txt.exe",
        "/r/report\u{202e}.txt",
        "/r/two\nlines.txt",
    ] {
        assert!(!may_launch(Path::new(refused)), "{refused:?} may launch");
    }
}

    assert!(!altered_by_windows("notes.txt"));
    #[cfg(windows)]

}

#[test]
fn open_launches_an_allowed_document_and_reveals_anything_else() {
    let folder = Folder::new();
    let notes = folder.file("notes.txt");
    let script = folder.file("run.sh");
    let bare = folder.file("Makefile");
    let mut desktop = Desktop::default();
    desktop.open(&notes);
    desktop.open(&script);
    desktop.open(&bare);
    desktop.reveal(&notes);
    assert_eq!(
        calls(desktop),
        [
            Call::Open(notes.clone()),
            Call::Reveal(script),
            Call::Reveal(bare),
            Call::Reveal(notes),
        ]
    );

crate::windows_unix_omission!(
    a_link_is_judged_by_where_it_leads,
    "unix-only: creates symlinks to distinguish a script link from a regular document"
);

/// A link is judged by the file it leads to: a `.txt` name pointing at a script is revealed, and
/// one pointing at a document opens that document.
#[cfg(unix)]
#[test]
fn a_link_is_judged_by_where_it_leads() {
    let folder = Folder::new();
    let script = folder.file("run.sh");
    let readme = folder.file("readme.txt");
    let disguised = folder.0.join("notes.txt");
    let honest = folder.0.join("about.txt");
    std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&script, &disguised).unwrap();
    std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&readme, &honest).unwrap();
    let mut desktop = Desktop::default();
    desktop.open(&disguised);
    desktop.open(&honest);
    assert_eq!(calls(desktop), [Call::Reveal(script), Call::Open(readme)]);
}

/// What `act` handed the desktop, and the line it would print.
fn acted(path: &Path, open: bool, answer: Result<(), String>) -> (Vec<Call>, Result<(), String>) {
    let mut calls = Vec::new();
    let line = act(path, open, |call| {
        calls.push(call.clone());
        answer
    });
    (calls, line)
}

/// The click's whole path, as the real desktop runs it: an allowed document reaches the launcher,
/// anything else reaches it as a reveal, and Open in File Manager never as an open.
#[test]
fn a_click_hands_the_desktop_what_it_decided() {
    let folder = Folder::new();
    let notes = folder.file("notes.txt");
    let script = folder.file("run.sh");
    assert_eq!(
        acted(&notes, true, Ok(())),
        (vec![Call::Open(notes.clone())], Ok(()))
    );
    assert_eq!(acted(&script, true, Ok(())).0, [Call::Reveal(script)]);
    assert_eq!(acted(&notes, false, Ok(())).0, [Call::Reveal(notes)]);
}

/// A failure names what was attempted: an Open that became a reveal could not show.
#[test]
fn a_failure_says_what_the_click_actually_tried() {
    let folder = Folder::new();
    let notes = folder.file("notes.txt");
    let script = folder.file("run.sh");
    let failed = |path: &Path| acted(path, true, Err("no handler".into())).1.unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(failed(&notes), could_not("open", &notes, "no handler"));
    assert_eq!(failed(&script), could_not("show", &script, "no handler"));
    let missing = folder.0.join("gone.txt");
    let (calls, line) = acted(&missing, true, Ok(()));
    assert!(calls.is_empty());
    assert!(line.unwrap_err().starts_with("ivy: could not open"));
}

/// Only a regular file opens: a folder named like a document is shown instead.
#[test]
fn only_a_regular_file_is_launched() {
    let folder = Folder::new();
    let named = folder.0.join("notes.txt");
    std::fs::create_dir(&named).unwrap();
    let document = folder.file("real.txt");
    assert_eq!(acted(&named, true, Ok(())).0, [Call::Reveal(named)]);
    assert_eq!(acted(&document, true, Ok(())).0, [Call::Open(document)]);
}

crate::windows_unix_omission!(
    a_file_swapped_for_a_link_after_the_judgement_is_not_launched,
    "unix-only: requires Unix symlink or permission semantics"
);

/// Right before the launch, the judged file is read again: once swapped for a link, it is not
/// handed over.
#[cfg(unix)]
#[test]
fn a_file_swapped_for_a_link_after_the_judgement_is_not_launched() {
    let folder = Folder::new();
    let notes = folder.file("notes.txt");
    let script = folder.file("run.sh");
    assert_eq!(still_a_file(&notes), Ok(()));
    std::fs::remove_file(&notes).unwrap();
    std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&script, &notes).unwrap();
    assert!(still_a_file(&notes).is_err());
    // A folder in its place is refused too; a regular file again passes.
    std::fs::remove_file(&notes).unwrap();
    std::fs::create_dir(&notes).unwrap();
    assert!(still_a_file(&notes).is_err());
    assert_eq!(still_a_file(&script), Ok(()));
}

#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
mod linux {
    use super::super::linux::{Launch, command_for, opener, wait};
    use super::Folder;
    use crate::opener::Call;
    use std::cell::RefCell;
    use std::path::Path;

    #[test]
    fn the_opener_gets_the_path_as_its_one_argument() {
        let path = Path::new("/r/a b; $(rm -rf x).txt");
        let command = opener(path);
        assert_eq!(command.get_program(), "xdg-open");
        assert_eq!(command.get_args().collect::<Vec<_>>(), [path.as_os_str()]);
    }

    fn args(command: &std::process::Command) -> Vec<&std::ffi::OsStr> {
        command.get_args().collect()
    }

    /// An open runs `xdg-open` on the file and never asks the file manager.
    #[test]
    fn an_open_runs_the_file_and_asks_no_bus() {
        let folder = Folder::new();
        let file = folder.file("notes.txt");
        let asked = RefCell::new(0);
        let launch = command_for(&Call::Open(file.clone()), |_| {
            *asked.borrow_mut() += 1;
            Ok(())
        })
        .unwrap();
        let Launch::Run(command) = launch else {
            panic!("an open runs a command")
        };
        assert_eq!(command.get_program(), "xdg-open");
        assert_eq!(args(&command), [file.as_os_str()]);
        assert_eq!(asked.into_inner(), 0);
    }

    /// The file manager is asked for the file itself; when it fails, its folder opens — never the
    /// file — and the reason travels with it.
    #[test]
    fn a_reveal_nobody_answers_opens_the_folder_and_never_the_file() {
        let folder = Folder::new();
        std::fs::create_dir(folder.0.join("src")).unwrap();
        let path = folder.file("src/my file.txt");
        let uri = url::Url::from_file_path(&path).unwrap().to_string();
        let asked = RefCell::new(Vec::new());
        let reveal = Call::Reveal(path.clone());
        let answered = command_for(&reveal, |uri| {
            asked.borrow_mut().push(uri.to_owned());
            Ok(())
        })
        .unwrap();
        assert!(matches!(answered, Launch::Done));
        let unanswered = command_for(&reveal, |uri| {
            asked.borrow_mut().push(uri.to_owned());
            Err("no service".into())
        })
        .unwrap();
        let Launch::Folder { command, why } = unanswered else {
            panic!("a folder to open instead")
        };
        assert_eq!(command.get_program(), "xdg-open");
        assert_eq!(args(&command), [folder.0.join("src").as_os_str()]);
        assert_eq!(why, "no service");
        assert_eq!(asked.into_inner(), [uri.clone(), uri]);
    }

    /// The folder a failed reveal falls back to is read again: a link in its place is not opened.
    #[test]
    fn a_folder_swapped_for_a_link_is_not_opened() {
        let folder = Folder::new();
        let page = folder.file("page.html");
        let swapped = folder.0.join("src");
        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&page, &swapped).unwrap();
        let reveal = Call::Reveal(swapped.join("a.txt"));
        let refused = command_for(&reveal, |_| Err("no service".into()));
        assert!(refused.is_err());
        std::fs::remove_file(&swapped).unwrap();
        std::fs::create_dir(&swapped).unwrap();
        assert!(matches!(
            command_for(&reveal, |_| Err("no service".into())),
            Ok(Launch::Folder { .. })
        ));
    }

    /// The launcher is waited for, and how it ended is what the click reports.
    #[test]
    fn a_launcher_that_exits_unhappy_says_so() {
        assert_eq!(wait(std::process::Command::new("true")), Ok(()));
        let failed = wait(std::process::Command::new("false")).unwrap_err();
        assert!(
            failed.contains("\"false\"") && failed.contains("exit status: 1"),
            "{failed}"
        );
    }
}
```

### crates/app/src/opening.rs

```
//! How a window comes into being: with the tabs it is asked for, or with the ones it left behind.
//!
//! **Three methods and one subject.** Building a window is not what a window *does* — it is what
//! somebody does to one — and it reads as its own vocabulary: how many tabs, which consoles, what
//! the session file said and what happens when it says nothing understandable. Everything about
//! running is in [`crate`]; everything about starting is here.
//!
//! It came out of the window's core under **D28**, which gives an `impl` block a ceiling of 300
//! production lines and records what that file is at so the number can only go down. **It went out
//! ahead of the delivery that needed the room** — the milestone that gives the panel buttons — which
//! is the order that entry's own prose asks for.

use crate::painter::CellMetrics;
use crate::*;
use eframe::egui;

#[cfg(all(test, unix))]
mod codex_tests;

#[cfg(all(test, windows))]
mod codex_tests {
    crate::windows_unix_omission!(
        launched_shells_keep_their_own_pane_identity_and_survive_repeated_codex_runs,
        "unix-only: launches POSIX shell wrapper scripts and relies on Unix executable permissions"
    );
}

pub(crate) fn native_window(
    cc: &eframe::CreationContext<'_>,
    with_what: OpenWith,
    size: Option<egui::Vec2>,
    persistence: session::Persistence,
    measuring: bool,
) -> term_core::Result<Window> {
    chrome::prepare(&cc.egui_ctx);
    let mut window = Window::opening(
        &cc.egui_ctx,
        with_what,
        &the_users_shell(),
        size,
        persistence,
    )?;
    if !measuring {
        window.clipboard = clipboard::Service::new(cc.egui_ctx.clone());
    }
    Ok(window)
}

impl Pane {
    pub(crate) fn new_counting_reads(
        ctx: &egui::Context,
        name: usize,
        reads: control::ReadActivity,
    ) -> term_core::Result<Self> {
        new_in_directory(ctx, name, reads, None)
    }

    #[cfg(test)]
    pub(crate) fn launching(
        ctx: &egui::Context,
        program: &str,
        name: usize,
    ) -> term_core::Result<Self> {
        launch_pane(
            ctx,
            program,
            &[],
            panel_or_none(PanelDir::create()),
            name,
            control::ReadActivity::default(),
        )
    }

    pub(crate) fn launching_with_args_counting_reads(
        ctx: &egui::Context,
        program: &str,
        args: &[String],
        name: usize,
        reads: control::ReadActivity,
    ) -> term_core::Result<Self> {
        launch_pane(
            ctx,
            program,
            args,
            panel_or_none(PanelDir::create()),
            name,
            reads,
        )
    }

    #[cfg(test)]
    pub(crate) fn with_panel(
        ctx: &egui::Context,
        program: &str,
        args: &[String],
        panel: Option<PanelDir>,
        name: usize,
    ) -> term_core::Result<Self> {
        launch_pane(
            ctx,
            program,
            args,
            panel,
            name,
            control::ReadActivity::default(),
        )
    }
}

impl Window {
    /// A window with as many tabs as it is asked for.
    ///
    /// **How many there are is told from outside and is not read from the environment here**, even
    /// though the only place that asks for more than one today is [`run`] reading
    /// [`TABS_VARIABLE`]. Reading it inside would let a variable exported in the session change
    /// what the tests that call here open —they run in the same process and at the same time—, and
    /// a test saying «the window opens with a single tab» would start failing depending on which
    /// other one happened to be running at that instant.
    ///
    /// **Asking for zero tabs is the caller's error, and it is not corrected here.** The floor is
    /// set by [`how_many_tabs`], which is where the only value we do not control comes in; a second
    /// guard here would only silently cover up a call that cannot happen. The honest thing would be
    /// a type that did not admit zero —`NonZeroUsize`—, and it is not part of this change because
    /// it forces a wrapper in every test that opens a window.
    pub(crate) fn with_tabs(
        ctx: &egui::Context,
        how_many: usize,
        layout: OpeningLayout,
        size: Option<egui::Vec2>,
    ) -> term_core::Result<Self> {
        // **If any one fails none is opened**, and the `?` over the `collect` is what achieves it:
        // the same criterion as `panel_or_none`. Half a dozen tabs of which one is half-built is
        // worse than not starting up at all, because the failure is discovered on reaching it.
        let per_tab = layout.how_many_panes();
        let control = control::WindowActivity::default();
        let reads = control.read_activity();
        let tabs: Vec<Tab> = (0..how_many)
            .map(|name| Tab::new_counting_reads(ctx, name, name * per_tab, &layout, reads.clone()))
            .collect::<term_core::Result<_>>()?;

        Self::with_these_tabs(ctx, tabs, size, control)
    }

    /// The window the application opens: factory-fresh, or with whatever was left saved.
    ///
    /// **And if restoring fails, it opens factory-fresh instead of not opening.** It is this
    /// format's per-document tolerance taken all the way: what can fail here is opening N consoles
    /// in one go —a descriptor, a process—, and against that «the usual window» is a much better
    /// answer than an application that does not start. Atomicity comes from the step below: if one
    /// leaf cannot be opened, **none is installed**, so half a layout never arrives here.
    pub(crate) fn opening(
        ctx: &egui::Context,
        with_what: OpenWith,
        program: &str,
        size: Option<egui::Vec2>,
        persistence: session::Persistence,
    ) -> term_core::Result<Self> {
        // **The program is received, and the branch that restores is the one that uses it.** The
        // factory branch opens the user's shell as it always has, just like the three dozen tests
        // that call it: passing it all the way down there would mean touching the signature of a
        // constructor with thirty-three calls for the one path this PR does not introduce. It is
        // said because the previous comment claimed that **no** branch read the environment, and
        // that was false precisely for the factory one — the two reviews caught it.
        let mut do_not_overwrite = None;
        let opened = match with_what {
            OpenWith::Factory { tabs, layout } => Self::with_tabs(ctx, tabs, layout, size),
            OpenWith::Restored(saved) => {
                let panes = saved.leaves();
                let control = control::WindowActivity::default();
                // **Opening them is what is timed, not reading the file, and the difference
                // matters.** What grows with the size of the layout are the `fork`s and `exec`s
                // here: reading a four-leaf document and a two-hundred-leaf one cost almost the
                // same. What reading cost travels with the persistence, which already knows how
                // long its own load took.
                let before = Instant::now();
                match Self::materialize_counting_reads(
                    ctx,
                    &saved,
                    program,
                    control.read_activity(),
                ) {
                    Ok(tabs) => {
                        Self::with_these_tabs(ctx, tabs, size, control).map(|mut window| {
                            // **Only when they really were opened.** If materializing fails it
                            // opens factory-fresh, and a factory window declaring «restored» would
                            // be the disguise this line exists to remove.
                            //
                            // **And the stopwatch closes HERE, with the window already built, not
                            // when the processes exist.** Behind `materialize` there are two jobs
                            // that **grow with the number of consoles** —arming each directory's
                            // watcher and describing the whole tree for the first mirror—, and
                            // stopping it earlier left them out of a number that claims to measure
                            // exactly that.
                            window.restoring = Some(measure::Restoring {
                                consoles: panes,
                                read: persistence.load_time(),
                                opening: before.elapsed(),
                            });
                            window
                        })
                    }
                    Err(failure) => {
                        eprintln!(
                            "the saved layout of {panes} consoles could not be opened ({failure}); \
                             opening the factory one, and what is saved is left alone"
                        );
                        // **And this session does not step on the file, which is what avoids losing
                        // the layout for ever.** What is left on screen is a factory window; with
                        // automatic saving on, the user's first change would write it over their
                        // twenty consoles. Before this milestone the file survived a failed
                        // start-up and the next one recovered it: this preserves that property.
                        do_not_overwrite = Some("the layout that was saved could not be opened");
                        Self::with_tabs(ctx, 1, OpeningLayout::DEFAULT, size)
                    }
                }
            }
        };

        // **The one that loaded at start-up stays with the window, and this was a debt with a
        // name.** The veto of a newer version lives in it: building a second one mid-session would
        // lose that veto and would overwrite the layout of a newer binary, which is the exact
        // damage the format version exists to avoid. It is installed here, after building, instead
        // of being pushed down through the three constructors: the factory one is called by
        // thirty-three tests that save nothing.
        opened.map(|mut window| {
            window.persistence = persistence;
            if let Some(reason) = do_not_overwrite {
                window.persistence.forbid_overwrite(reason);
            }
            window
        })
    }

    /// The rest of the window, which is the same whether it was restored or not.
    ///
    /// **It exists so that opening factory-fresh and restoring share everything that is not the tab
    /// list.** The counters, the watcher and the stopwatches come **from the built list** and not
    /// from what anyone might have asked for: with two paths arming this state on their own, the
    /// second would forget one of them.
    pub(crate) fn with_these_tabs(
        ctx: &egui::Context,
        tabs: Vec<Tab>,
        size: Option<egui::Vec2>,
        control: control::WindowActivity,
    ) -> term_core::Result<Self> {
        let mut window = initial_window(ctx, tabs, size, control);

        // **The window is described once when it is born, and with that the deadline does not arm
        // itself.** Without this, the first frame's mirror differs from «nothing» and every session
        // would write the file half a second after opening, without the user having arranged
        // anything. The last thing **written** is left as nothing on purpose: what is on disk may
        // not be exactly this —a width below the floor comes back clipped—, so the first real
        // change rewrites the whole file and brings it up to date.
        window.last_seen = Some(window.describe());

        Ok(window)
    }
}

/// Initial state shared by fresh and restored windows; the caller records the first layout mirror.
fn initial_window(
    ctx: &egui::Context,
    tabs: Vec<Tab>,
    size: Option<egui::Vec2>,
    control: control::WindowActivity,
) -> Window {
    let next_name = tabs.len();
    // **Counted from the list and not from the parameter**, for the same reason explained below
    // for the tab name: they are two sources for the same datum, and with two you can take the
    // wrong one.
    let next_pane_name = tabs.iter().map(|tab| tab.panes().len()).sum();

    Window {
        // **Over the panes of every tab, not just the first.** A background tab left unwatched
        // would not reread its panel until someone looked at it, which is exactly the opposite
        // of what this milestone promises.
        watcher: Window::watch(ctx, tabs.iter().flat_map(|tab| tab.panes().into_iter())),
        tabs,
        active: 0,
        modifiers: egui::Modifiers::NONE,
        discoverer: fonts::Discoverer::default(),
        discovery: measure::Discovery::default(),
        color: measure::Rasterizing::default(),
        searching: measure::Searching::default(),
        theme: Theme::default(),
        keymap: keyboard::Keymap::default(),
        copy_enabled: true,
        ligatures: false,
        ligature_measurement: None,
        blink: alarms::BlinkState::default(),
        font_size: DEFAULT_FONT_SIZE,
        rest_of_frame: RestOfFrame::default(),
        frame_started: Instant::now(),
        last_closed_frame: 0,
        timed_separately: std::time::Duration::ZERO,
        tab_bar_this_frame: std::time::Duration::ZERO,
        tab_bar_cost: measure::TabBar::default(),
        strips: crate::report::Strips::default(),
        buttons: measure::Buttons::default(),
        links: measure::Links::default(),
        desktop: crate::links::Desktop::System,
        control,
        reflow: measure::Reflow::default(),
        launch_cost: Default::default(),
        persistence: session::Persistence::at(None),
        restoring: None,
        reading_settings: measure::ReadingSettings::default(),
        chosen_font: measure::ChoosingTheFont::default(),
        last_seen: None,
        mirror_this_frame: std::time::Duration::ZERO,
        saving: measure::Saving::default(),
        // **No input at all, and whoever measures is the one who puts it there.** It used to be
        // one more parameter of this constructor, and the review threw it out with an argument
        // the tests themselves already proved: nobody but a batch sets it, and in exchange the
        // signature ended in two consecutive `Option`s filled with two bare `None`s in more
        // than thirty calls — two slots that do not say which is which.
        injected_drags: None,
        spent_injecting: std::time::Duration::ZERO,
        // **Painted unless whoever opens the window says otherwise**, which is what
        // [`crate::run`] does for a measurement batch: a constructor that decided it by reading
        // the environment would make every test depend on what the session that launched it
        // exported.
        headers: crate::header::Headers::PAINTED,
        chord_of_the_last_press: egui::Modifiers::NONE,
        where_the_last_press_fell: None,
        the_press_went_to_the_program: false,
        presses_for_the_program: Vec::new(),
        last_cell_reported: None,
        last_frame_with_buttons: None,
        header_input: crate::header::actions::Input::default(),
        settings_editor: crate::settings::editor::Editor::default(),
        chrome: crate::chrome::Chrome::default(),
        pane_view: crate::pane_view::State::default(),
        clipboard: crate::clipboard::Service::default(),
        board: crate::board::State::default(),
        sidebar: crate::sidebar::Sidebar::default(),
        viewer_other: crate::viewer::State::default(),
        viewer: crate::viewer::State::default(),
        explorer: crate::explorer::Explorer::default(),
        projects: crate::project_view::State::default(),
        restoration: crate::restoration::State::default(),
        link_press_in_progress: None,
        drag_in_progress: None,
        last_painted: Vec::new(),
        // **From the length of the list and not from the `panes` parameter, even though here
        // they are worth the same.** They are two sources for the same datum, and with two you
        // can take the wrong one: putting a zero or the wrong parameter makes the next tab that
        // opens **be called the same as one that already exists**, and the report publish two
        // blocks of numbers under the same label. Checked by mutating it, and no test catches
        // it: this constructor launches real consoles reading `SHELL`. Since restoring exists
        // **a test does catch it**: the one that opens with two saved tabs asserts both
        // counters. Deriving it from the list is what leaves nothing to confuse.
        next_name,
        next_pane_name,
        some_closed_by_hand: false,
        // Until the first frame there is no window to measure. A zero is what is really known,
        // and the report is only published once some console got to interpret something — that
        // is, after several frames.
        window_size: egui::Vec2::ZERO,
        requested_size: size.map(|how_much| RequestedSize {
            how_much,
            attempts: FRAMES_INSISTING_ON_SIZE,
        }),
        opened_by_agents: control::OpenedByAgents::default(),
    }
}

/// What a keyboard chord asks of the window, when what it asks is not the console's.
///
/// **It comes out through [`Pane::send_keys`]'s return value and not through an output parameter**,
/// because it is output only: that function's `&mut Modifiers` is a parameter because it **is also
/// input** —the chord can start on one frame and be used on the next—, and a request drags nothing
/// from the previous pass.
///
/// **It lives here and not in the crate's core** because applying one is what somebody asks a window
/// to do, which is this module's subject. It came out under **D28** ahead of the delivery that gives
/// an agent the same gestures over the channel, which is what pays the lines the core spends back.
#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Clone, Copy)]
pub(crate) enum WindowRequest {
    /// Change tab, **as many as the number says**: negative backwards, positive forwards.
    ///
    /// **It carries a number and not a direction because clicking a card jumps wherever**: from the
    /// first to the third is two. Each key press, on the other hand, is worth exactly one — they do
    /// not compose with each other, they are applied in order, and why it is like that is in
    /// [`Pane::send_keys`].
    Go(isize),
    /// Open a new tab, with its two consoles, and leave it in front.
    NewTab,
    /// Close the tab being looked at. If it is the only one, what is closed is the window.
    CloseTab,
    /// Split in two the pane that has the focus, cutting in that direction.
    Split(Orientation),
    /// Close the pane that has the focus, and let its siblings share out its place.
    ///
    /// **It carries the suffix, and so do its two sisters above.** Since both things can be closed,
    /// a bare «close» next to a «close the pane» would be told apart only by what **one of the two
    /// does not say**, and getting it wrong kills the programs of a whole tab instead of one. For
    /// the same reason [`WindowRequest::NewTab`] says what it opens: if some day something that is
    /// not a tab can be opened, the name that did not say so would become the ambiguous one of the
    /// two.
    ClosePane,
}

impl Window {
    /// Does what the chord asked for, and **asks for a frame**.
    ///
    /// The second is not a precaution: today the only ones that ask for a repaint are the pending
    /// queue, the blind watcher's fallback and the panels' alarm clock. With two quiet tabs —two
    /// agents waiting for an answer, which is when this is used— none of the three has anything to
    /// ask for, so without this line the window would go to sleep **showing the previous tab** and
    /// the chord would look as though it had not worked. `whole_window` and `metrics` are where the
    /// geometry **splitting** needs comes from: how big the focused pane is has to be known in
    /// order to be able to refuse when it does not fit. They are passed instead of stored because
    /// the area belongs to the interface pass and this can run several times inside one —the same
    /// batch of events can bring several chords—, and **the bar appears or disappears along the
    /// way**: creating the second tab takes height away from the consoles at that very instant, so
    /// the area is recomputed one request at a time and not once for all of them.
    pub(crate) fn apply(
        &mut self,
        request: WindowRequest,
        ctx: &egui::Context,
        whole_window: egui::Rect,
        metrics: &CellMetrics,
    ) {
        if matches!(request, WindowRequest::Split(_) | WindowRequest::ClosePane) {
            let tab = self.active().name;
            self.pane_view.clear(tab);
        }
        match request {
            // The wrap-around comes from `rem_euclid`, which unlike the usual remainder **does not
            // return negatives**: from the first one backwards you reach the last, with no separate
            // branch for that case.
            WindowRequest::Go(delta) => {
                let tabs = self.tabs.len() as isize;
                self.active = (self.active as isize + delta).rem_euclid(tabs) as usize;
            }
            WindowRequest::NewTab => self.create_a_tab(ctx),
            WindowRequest::CloseTab => self.close_the_active_tab(ctx),
            WindowRequest::Split(cut_along) => {
                self.split_the_focused(cut_along, ctx, whole_window, metrics)
            }
            WindowRequest::ClosePane => self.close_the_focused(ctx),
        }

        ctx.request_repaint();
    }
}

/// What size to open the window with, from what the variable said.
///
/// **Nothing instead of a default size**: with no variable nothing is asked for and the desktop
/// decides, which is what it does today and what it has to go on doing for whoever uses the
/// application.
///
/// **A value that is not understood is ignored just as if it were not there**, as in
/// [`how_many_tabs`]: aborting the startup over a badly written variable would be worse, and
/// whoever measures finds out as soon as they compare the size they asked for with the one the
/// report publishes. What is not a number, what is not finite and what is not positive are rejected
/// — a width of zero leaves a window with nothing inside and a `NaN` propagates to all the
/// painting.
pub(crate) fn window_size(value: Option<&str>) -> Option<egui::Vec2> {
    let (width, height) = value?.split_once('x')?;
    let width: f32 = width.trim().parse().ok()?;
    let height: f32 = height.trim().parse().ok()?;

    (width.is_finite() && width > 0.0 && height.is_finite() && height > 0.0)
        .then_some(egui::vec2(width, height))
}

/// What the environment variable says, already resolved.
pub(crate) fn size_at_startup() -> Option<egui::Vec2> {
    window_size(std::env::var(SIZE_VARIABLE).ok().as_deref())
}

/// Opens the PTY, output bridge and producer endpoint as one pane.
pub(crate) fn new_in_directory(
    ctx: &egui::Context,
    name: usize,
    reads: control::ReadActivity,
    directory: Option<&std::path::Path>,
) -> term_core::Result<Pane> {
    launch_shell_in(
        ctx,
        &the_users_shell(),
        panel_or_none(PanelDir::create()),
        name,
        reads,
        directory,
    )
}

pub(crate) fn prepare_shell(program: &str) -> Option<shell_integration::Prepared> {
    let helper = shell_integration::helper_path();
    match shell_integration::Prepared::prepare(program, helper.as_deref()) {
        Ok(prepared) => prepared,
        Err(error) => {
            eprintln!("Ivy's automatic Codex integration could not be prepared: {error}");
            None
        }
    }
}

pub(crate) fn requested_console(
    ctx: &egui::Context,
    program: Option<&str>,
    args: &[String],
    name: usize,
    reads: control::ReadActivity,
) -> term_core::Result<Pane> {
    if program.is_none() && args.is_empty() {
        Pane::new_counting_reads(ctx, name, reads)
    } else {
        Pane::launching_with_args_counting_reads(
            ctx,
            program.unwrap_or(&the_users_shell()),
            args,
            name,
            reads,
        )
    }
}

pub(crate) fn launch_shell_in(
    ctx: &egui::Context,
    program: &str,
    panel: Option<PanelDir>,
    name: usize,
    reads: control::ReadActivity,
    directory: Option<&std::path::Path>,
) -> term_core::Result<Pane> {
    let integration = prepare_shell(program);
    launch(
        ctx,
        Launch {
            program,
            args: &[],
            directory,
            path: None,
        },
        panel,
        name,
        reads,
        true,
        integration,
    )
}

fn launch_pane(
    ctx: &egui::Context,
    program: &str,
    args: &[String],
    panel: Option<PanelDir>,
    name: usize,
    reads: control::ReadActivity,
) -> term_core::Result<Pane> {
    launch_pane_in(ctx, program, args, panel, name, reads, None)
}

pub(crate) fn launch_pane_in(
    ctx: &egui::Context,
    program: &str,
    args: &[String],
    panel: Option<PanelDir>,
    name: usize,
    reads: control::ReadActivity,
    directory: Option<&std::path::Path>,
) -> term_core::Result<Pane> {
    launch(
        ctx,
        Launch {
            program,
            args,
            directory,
            path: None,
        },
        panel,
        name,
        reads,
        true,
        None,
    )
}

pub(crate) struct Launch<'a> {
    pub(crate) program: &'a str,
    pub(crate) args: &'a [String],
    pub(crate) directory: Option<&'a std::path::Path>,
    pub(crate) path: Option<&'a std::ffi::OsStr>,
}

pub(crate) fn launch_exact(
    ctx: &egui::Context,
    program: Launch<'_>,
    panel: Option<PanelDir>,
    name: usize,
    reads: control::ReadActivity,
) -> term_core::Result<Pane> {
    launch(ctx, program, panel, name, reads, false, None)
}

fn launch(
    ctx: &egui::Context,
    program: Launch<'_>,
    panel: Option<PanelDir>,
    name: usize,
    reads: control::ReadActivity,
    fallback: bool,
    integration: Option<shell_integration::Prepared>,
) -> term_core::Result<Pane> {
    let Launch {
        program,
        args,
        directory,
        path,
    } = program;
    let args = integration
        .as_ref()
        .map_or(args, |prepared| prepared.args());
    let mut identity: Vec<_> = identity_of(panel.as_ref()).into_iter().collect();
    let helper = crate::resume::helper_path();
    identity.push((
        "IVY_SESSION_HELPER",
        helper.as_deref().map(std::path::Path::as_os_str),
    ));
    if let Some(path) = path {
        identity.push(("PATH", Some(path)));
    }
    if let Some(prepared) = &integration {
        identity.extend(prepared.env());
    }
    let borrowed: Vec<&str> = args.iter().map(String::as_str).collect();
    let launch_waker = ctx.clone();
    let (mut pty, output) = crate::launch::Process::start(
        std::sync::Arc::new(move || launch_waker.request_repaint()),
        reads.clone(),
        crate::launch::Request {
            program,
            args: &borrowed,
            size: INITIAL_GRID,
            env: &identity,
            directory,
            fallback,
        },
    )?;

    pty.context = Some(ctx.clone());

    #[cfg(test)]
    crate::tests::completed_operation(crate::tests::OperationWork::Launch);

    let waker = ctx.clone();
    let wake: std::sync::Arc<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync> =
        std::sync::Arc::new(move || waker.request_repaint());
    let output_wake = std::sync::Arc::clone(&wake);
    let pty_output = PtyOutput::new(output, move || output_wake());
    let mut restoration = crate::restoration::PaneState {
        wake: Some(wake),
        ..Default::default()
    };
    let callback = restoration.route.callback();
    // Diagnostic attribution has its own lifetime and is installed regardless of whether session
    // restoration is enabled. The listener starts below, after the PTY root already exists.
    let provenance_inspector = pty
        .foreground_scope()
        .map(panel::provenance_route::inspector)
        .unwrap_or_else(|_| panel::provenance_route::unavailable());
    let (panel_state, control) = if pty.pending() {
        (panel::PanelState::default(), None)
    } else {
        panel::pane::state_and_control_with_authority(
            ctx,
            panel.as_ref(),
            reads,
            Some(callback),
            pty.foreground_scope()
                .ok()
                .map(crate::panel::overlay::Authority::new),
            provenance_inspector,
        )
    };

    Ok(Pane {
        signals: Default::default(),
        name,
        label: None,
        project: None,
        program: None,
        restoration,
        pty,
        pty_output,
        _shell_integration: integration,
        panel,
        control,
        panel_state,
        to_reread: Flag::raised(),
        screen: Screen::new(INITIAL_GRID),
        painted_console: None,
        received: Vec::new(),
        keystrokes: Vec::new(),
        held: None,
        search: crate::search::OnThisConsole::default(),
        measured: INITIAL_GRID,
        timings: Timings::default(),
        pending_measurement: None,
        panel_reread_unpainted: std::time::Duration::ZERO,
        control_unpainted: std::time::Duration::ZERO,
        reflowed: std::time::Duration::ZERO,
        pending_wheel: WheelInput::default(),
        panel_width: panel::DEFAULT_WIDTH,
        folded: false,
        taken_over: None,
    })
}

/// Which layout the environment variable asks for, if it asks for a valid one.
///
/// **A value that is not understood leaves the one of always**, just as in [`how_many_tabs`]:
/// whoever measures finds out as soon as they see two consoles where they asked for four, and
/// aborting the startup over a badly written variable would be worse. A zero in either of the two
/// axes is not a layout: there would be no console at all.
pub(crate) fn opening_layout_of(value: Option<&str>) -> OpeningLayout {
    let requested = value.and_then(|value| {
        let (columns, rows) = value.split_once('x')?;

        OpeningLayout::new(columns.trim().parse().ok()?, rows.trim().parse().ok()?)
    });

    requested.unwrap_or(OpeningLayout::DEFAULT)
}

/// How many tabs the window opens with, from what the variable said.
///
/// **Never fewer than one**, and that floor is not caution: the list of tabs cannot be empty —the
/// window always shows one— and a zero would leave every access indexing out of range on the first
/// frame. A value that is not a number is ignored just as if it were not there: whoever measures
/// finds out on seeing a single tab, and aborting the startup over a badly written variable would
/// be worse.
///
/// **It receives the value and does not read it, and a review decided that.** Reading it here would
/// force the tests to call `std::env::set_var`, whose contract is over **all** the environment: any
/// concurrent read is undefined behavior, not only the one of this key. And in this suite there
/// are plenty of them — `Pane::new` reads `SHELL`, and `portable_pty` walks `std::env::vars_os()`
/// when building **every** console—, with the tests running in threads at the same time. The only
/// line left uncovered is [`run`]'s, for the same reason as `on_exit`'s.
pub(crate) fn how_many_tabs(value: Option<&str>) -> usize {
    value
        .and_then(|value| value.parse().ok())
        .unwrap_or(1)
        .max(1)
}

/// Launch a fresh tab and declare launch plus watcher activation as one operation.
pub(crate) fn create_a_tab(window: &mut Window, ctx: &egui::Context) {
    // Launching the console and activating its watchers are declared together.
    // Every new tab starts with one console; window restore and measurement layouts
    // retain the shape they were explicitly given.
    let before = Instant::now();
    #[cfg(test)]
    let before = crate::tests::OperationClock::start(before);
    let with_what = OpeningLayout {
        columns: 1,
        rows: 1,
    };
    let opened = Tab::new_counting_reads(
        ctx,
        window.next_name,
        window.next_pane_name,
        &with_what,
        window.control.read_activity(),
    );
    // **The names are spent even if the opening fails**, so that they are never reused: if the
    // failed attempt left a line on the error output, whatever is opened afterwards must not be
    // called the same as what never got to exist. It holds for the tab and for its console
    // for the same reason, so both counters are advanced in the same place.
    window.next_name += 1;
    window.next_pane_name += with_what.how_many_panes();

    // **The stopwatch is closed after putting the tab in front, and that line was drawn in the
    // wrong place.** Putting it in front **rearms the watcher**, which rebuilds the watching
    // over the panes of every tab and makes them reread their `panel.json`: it is the expensive
    // half of opening a tab, and no pane declares it. Closing the stopwatch earlier, that work
    // fell into «the rest of the frame» — the name this project gives to the interface
    // framework cost, which is the figure D3 decides with. It is the same fix splitting already
    // carries.
    window.bring_to_front(opened, ctx);
    window.timed_separately += before.elapsed();
}

pub(crate) fn chrome_viewport(
    builder: egui::ViewportBuilder,
    enabled: bool,
) -> egui::ViewportBuilder {
    chrome_viewport_on(builder, enabled, cfg!(target_os = "macos"))
}

// The platform is a parameter so a Linux test can see the macOS branch.
fn chrome_viewport_on(
    builder: egui::ViewportBuilder,
    enabled: bool,
    macos: bool,
) -> egui::ViewportBuilder {
    if !enabled {
        builder
    } else if macos {
        builder
            .with_fullsize_content_view(true)
            .with_titlebar_shown(false)
            // A transparent titlebar still draws the title: the header paints its own.
            .with_title_shown(false)
    } else {
        builder.with_decorations(false)
    }
}

/// Constructs the pane watcher with the other window resources.
pub(crate) fn watch_panes<'a>(
    ctx: &egui::Context,
    panes: impl IntoIterator<Item = &'a Pane>,
) -> Watcher {
    let consoles: Vec<_> = panes
        .into_iter()
        .filter_map(|pane| {
            let dir = pane.panel.as_ref()?;
            Some((dir.path(), pane.to_reread.clone()))
        })
        .collect();

    // The only thing the watcher needs from the window is how to wake it, just like the bridge:
    // that way its module does not depend on the interface framework, which is what is going to
    // be rewritten. Asking for the repaint does not panic, which is the condition for whoever
    // calls it from another thread.
    let waker = ctx.clone();

    Watcher::new(move || waker.request_repaint(), &consoles)
}

pub(crate) fn insist_on_size(window: &mut Window, ctx: &egui::Context) {
    let Some(requested) = &mut window.requested_size else {
        return;
    };

    let how_much = requested.how_much;
    if (window.window_size - how_much).abs().max_elem() <= SIZE_TOLERANCE {
        return;
    }

    if requested.attempts == 0 {
        window.requested_size = None;
        // **It is said out loud, and on the error output of the process that measures.**
        // Whoever takes the batch has to know that the size they asked for is not the one there
        // was; the report publishes the one there was, so between the two things no room is
        // left to believe a measurement that cannot be reproduced.
        //
        // **And it is timed**, even though it happens only once in the session: writing to the
        // error output costs whatever whoever reads at the other end of the pipe takes, happens
        // inside the frame's pass and no pane declares it. It is the same as the two warnings
        // for splitting and closing already do.
        let before = Instant::now();
        eprintln!(
            "the desktop is not honouring the size asked for ({}x{} points): the window stays at {}x{} \
                 and the batches will not be comparable with other machines",
            how_much.x, how_much.y, window.window_size.x, window.window_size.y
        );
        window.timed_separately += before.elapsed();
        return;
    }

    requested.attempts -= 1;
    ctx.send_viewport_cmd(egui::ViewportCommand::InnerSize(how_much));
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn viewport(enabled: bool, macos: bool) -> egui::ViewportBuilder {
        chrome_viewport_on(egui::ViewportBuilder::default(), enabled, macos)
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_mac_window_hides_its_own_title_under_the_integrated_header() {
        let mac = viewport(true, true);
        assert_eq!(mac.title_shown, Some(false));
        assert_eq!(mac.titlebar_shown, Some(false));
        assert_eq!(mac.fullsize_content_view, Some(true));
        assert_eq!(mac.decorations, None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn other_platforms_drop_the_decorations_and_leave_the_mac_titlebar_alone() {
        let other = viewport(true, false);
        assert_eq!(other.decorations, Some(false));
        assert_eq!(other.title_shown, None);
        assert_eq!(other.titlebar_shown, None);
        assert_eq!(other.fullsize_content_view, None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn without_the_integrated_header_the_window_keeps_its_native_frame() {
        for macos in [false, true] {
            assert_eq!(viewport(false, macos), egui::ViewportBuilder::default());
        }
    }
}
```

### crates/app/src/panel/overlay.rs

```
//! A hook may repaint only the live client invocation that owns the current hold.

use panel_contract::{Attention, Panel};
use panel_control::{Connection, Delivery, Reply, What, session::Peer};
use std::sync::mpsc::{SyncSender, sync_channel};
use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex, OnceLock};
use std::time::SystemTime;
use term_core::ForegroundScope;

#[derive(Clone)]
struct Witness {
    #[cfg(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos"))]
    root: crate::client_attestation::Root,
}

impl Witness {
    fn current(&self, scope: &ForegroundScope) -> bool {
        #[cfg(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos"))]
        return self.root.current(scope);
        #[cfg(not(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos")))]
        {
            let _ = scope;
            false
        }
    }

    fn same(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
        #[cfg(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos"))]
        return self.root.same_invocation(&other.root);
        #[cfg(not(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos")))]
        {
            let _ = other;
            false
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone)]
struct Hold {
    connection: Connection,
    incarnation: u64,
    source: String,
    root: Option<Witness>,
}

#[derive(Default)]
struct Snapshot {
    incarnation: u64,
    hold: Option<Hold>,
}

pub(super) struct HoldCell(Arc<Mutex<Snapshot>>, bool);

impl Default for HoldCell {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self(Arc::default(), true)
    }
}

impl Clone for HoldCell {
    fn clone(&self) -> Self {
        Self(Arc::clone(&self.0), false)
    }
}

impl Drop for HoldCell {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if self.1 {
            self.clear();
        }
    }
}

impl HoldCell {
    pub(super) fn replace(&self, connection: Connection, source: &str) {
        let mut state = self.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
        state.incarnation = state
            .incarnation
            .checked_add(1)
            .expect("hold incarnation exhausted");
        state.hold = Some(Hold {
            connection,
            incarnation: state.incarnation,
            source: source.to_owned(),
            root: None,
        });
    }

    pub(super) fn clear(&self) {
        self.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).hold = None;
    }

    fn snapshot(&self) -> Option<Hold> {
        self.0
            .lock()
            .unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner())
            .hold
            .clone()
    }
}

pub(crate) struct Authority {
    scope: ForegroundScope,
    #[cfg(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos"))]
    programs: OnceLock<crate::client_attestation::Programs>,
}

impl Authority {
    pub(crate) fn new(scope: ForegroundScope) -> Arc<Self> {
        Arc::new(Self {
            scope,
            #[cfg(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos"))]
            programs: OnceLock::new(),
        })
    }

    fn prepare(&self) {
        #[cfg(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos"))]
        self.programs
            .get_or_init(crate::client_attestation::Programs::discover);
    }

    fn attest(&self, peer: &Peer) -> Option<Witness> {
        #[cfg(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos"))]
        return self
            .programs
            .get()?
            .root(&self.scope, peer)
            .ok()
            .map(|root| Witness { root });
        #[cfg(not(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos")))]
        {
            let _ = peer;
            None
        }
    }

    pub(crate) fn capture(&self, state: &super::PanelState, connection: Connection, peer: &Peer) {
        let root = self.attest(peer);
        let mut snapshot = state.overlay.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
        if let Some(hold) = &mut snapshot.hold
            && hold.connection == connection
        {
            hold.root = root;
        }
    }
}

struct Proof {
    root: Witness,
    authority: Arc<Authority>,
}

impl std::fmt::Debug for Proof {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.write_str("holding client invocation")
    }
}

impl panel_control::listener::OverlayAuthority for Proof {
    fn current(&self) -> bool {
        self.root.current(&self.authority.scope)
    }
}

pub(crate) fn service(
    state: &super::PanelState,
    authority: Arc<Authority>,
    sender: SyncSender<Delivery>,
    wake: Arc<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>,
) -> panel_control::listener::PublicationServices {
    let hold = state.overlay.clone();
    let verifier = Arc::clone(&authority);
    panel_control::listener::PublicationServices {
        publish: Arc::new(
            move |peer: Peer, _connection: Connection, document: String| {
                admit(&hold, &verifier, &sender, &peer, &document, &*wake)
            },
        ),
        prepare_holder: Arc::new(move || authority.prepare()),
    }
}

fn refused(code: &str, message: impl Into<String>) -> Reply {
    Reply::PublishRefused {
        code: code.to_owned(),
        message: message.into(),
    }
}

fn admit(
    cell: &HoldCell,
    authority: &Arc<Authority>,
    sender: &SyncSender<Delivery>,
    peer: &Peer,
    document: &str,
    wake: &dyn Fn(),
) -> Reply {
    let Some(hold) = cell.snapshot() else {
        return Reply::PublishUnheld;
    };
    let Some(root) = &hold.root else {
        return refused(
            "holder_unattested",
            format!(
                "this panel is held by \"{}\", which is not an attested agent client",
                hold.source
            ),
        );
    };
    let Some(publisher) = authority.attest(peer) else {
        return refused(
            "unauthorized",
            "publisher does not descend from this panel's holding client",
        );
    };
    if !root.same(&publisher) || !root.current(&authority.scope) {
        return refused(
            "unauthorized",
            "publisher does not descend from this panel's holding client",
        );
    }
    let panel = match panel_contract::parse_document(document) {
        Ok(panel) => panel,
        Err(why) => return refused("invalid_document", why.to_string()),
    };
    let proof = Arc::new(Proof {
        root: root.clone(),
        authority: Arc::clone(authority),
    });
    let (answer, _) = sync_channel(1);
    match sender.try_send(Delivery {
        origin: panel_control::PublicationOrigin::Unknown,
        connection: hold.connection,
        what: What::Overlay {
            panel,
            holder: hold.connection,
            incarnation: hold.incarnation,
            authority: proof,
        },
        answer,
    }) {
        Ok(()) => {
            wake();
            Reply::PublishAccepted
        }
        Err(_) => refused("queue_full", "this panel's publication queue is full"),
    }
}

impl super::PanelState {
    pub(crate) fn accepts_overlay(&self, connection: Connection, incarnation: u64) -> bool {
        self.overlay
            .snapshot()
            .is_some_and(|hold| hold.connection == connection && hold.incarnation == incarnation)
    }

    pub(crate) fn apply_overlay(&mut self, hook: Panel, now: SystemTime) -> Result<(), String> {
        if !self.channel_owns_document() {
            return Err(
                "this console's panel is its file's: publish with panel_set before patching"
                    .to_owned(),
            );
        }
        let mut merged = self.last_good.as_deref().ok_or("no held document")?.clone();
        merged.lines = hook.lines;
        merged.attention = hook.attention;
        merged.phase = hook.phase;
        merged.updated_at = hook.updated_at;
        merged.stale_after = hook.stale_after;
        merged.written_at = Some(now);
        if merged.attention != Some(Attention::Wait) {
            merged.actions.clear();
        }
        panel_contract::serialize(&merged).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        self.hook_source = self.held_by().map(|source| format!("{source} · hook"));
        super::driving::record_channel_write(self, &merged, now);
        self.commit(merged);
        self.problem = None;
        self.layout = None;
        self.drawn = None;
        Ok(())
    }
}

pub(crate) fn refused_at_apply(state: &mut super::PanelState) {
    // The admission reply cannot describe a later refusal, so the pane must confess it.
    state.hook_source = state
        .held_by()
        .map(|source| format!("{source} · hook update refused"));
    state.layout = None;
}

#[cfg(all(test, target_os = "linux"))]
#[path = "overlay/tests.rs"]
mod native_tests;

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use panel_contract::parse_document;

    fn held() -> super::super::PanelState {
        let mut state = super::super::PanelState::default();
        state.deliver(7, "flow", parse_document(r#"{"header":false,"milestone":"m","deliverable":"d","attention":"wait","actions":[{"label":"Approve","send":"yes"}],"lines":[{"key":"old","text":"Before"}]}"#).unwrap(), SystemTime::now()).unwrap();
        state
    }

    #[test]
    fn overlay_clears_wait_and_buttons_but_keeps_holder_metadata() {
        let mut state = held();
        let hook = parse_document(r#"{"header":true,"milestone":"attack","phase":"build","actions":[{"label":"Other","send":"evil"}],"lines":["After"]}"#).unwrap();
        state.apply_overlay(hook, SystemTime::now()).unwrap();
        let panel = state.last_good.as_deref().unwrap();
        assert!(!panel.header);
        assert_eq!(panel.milestone.as_deref(), Some("m"));
        assert_eq!(panel.deliverable.as_deref(), Some("d"));
        assert!(panel.attention.is_none());
        assert!(panel.actions.is_empty());
        assert_eq!(state.held_by(), Some("flow"));
        assert_eq!(state.displayed_source(), Some("flow · hook"));
        assert!(state.attention(SystemTime::now()).is_none());
        assert!(state.document().unwrap().contains("After"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn waiting_overlay_keeps_only_current_holder_actions_and_patch_uses_overlay() {
        let mut state = held();
        state.apply_overlay(parse_document(r#"{"attention":"wait","lines":[{"key":"new","text":"After"}],"actions":[{"label":"Inject","send":"evil"}]}"#).unwrap(), SystemTime::now()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(state.last_good.as_deref().unwrap().actions[0].send(), "yes");
        let ops = panel_contract::parse_ops(
            &serde_json::from_str(
                r#"[{"op":"replace","key":"new","line":{"key":"new","text":"Patched"}}]"#,
            )
            .unwrap(),
        );
        let ops = ops.unwrap();
        state.patch(7, &ops, SystemTime::now()).unwrap();
        assert!(state.document().unwrap().contains("Patched"));
        assert!(!state.document().unwrap().contains("Before"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn new_set_release_and_retire_invalidate_the_queued_hold_generation() {
        let mut state = held();
        let initial = state.overlay.snapshot().unwrap();
        assert!(state.accepts_overlay(7, initial.incarnation));
        state
            .deliver(
                7,
                "flow",
                parse_document(r#"{"lines":["Again"]}"#).unwrap(),
                SystemTime::now(),
            )
            .unwrap();
        assert!(!state.accepts_overlay(7, initial.incarnation));
        assert_eq!(state.displayed_source(), Some("flow"));
        let current = state.overlay.snapshot().unwrap();
        state.release(7);
        assert!(!state.accepts_overlay(7, current.incarnation));
        assert!(state.document().unwrap().contains("Again"));
        state
            .deliver(
                7,
                "flow",
                parse_document(r#"{"lines":["Last"]}"#).unwrap(),
                SystemTime::now(),
            )
            .unwrap();
        let last = state.overlay.snapshot().unwrap();
        let dir = panel_contract::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let baseline = super::super::PanelState::retirement_baseline(dir.path()).unwrap();
        state
            .retire(7, baseline, super::super::Retirement::default())
            .unwrap();
        assert!(!state.accepts_overlay(7, last.incarnation));
        assert!(state.document().is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn merged_result_ceiling_refuses_atomically() {
        let mut state = held();
        let before = state.document().unwrap();
        let mut hook = parse_document(r#"{"attention":"wait","lines":["x"]}"#).unwrap();
        // A legal hook can exceed the ceiling once holder metadata and buttons are retained.
        let filler = "x".repeat(panel_contract::MAX_BYTES as usize - 55);
        let text = serde_json::json!({"attention":"wait","lines":[filler]}).to_string();
        assert!(text.len() <= panel_contract::MAX_BYTES as usize);
        hook.lines = parse_document(&text).unwrap().lines;
        assert!(state.apply_overlay(hook, SystemTime::now()).is_err());
        assert_eq!(state.document().unwrap(), before);
    }

    #[test]
    fn apply_refuses_stale_or_revoked_authority_and_reports_each_once() {
        #[derive(Debug)]
        struct Current(bool);
        impl panel_control::OverlayAuthority for Current {
            fn current(&self) -> bool {
                self.0
            }
        }
        let mut state = held();
        let old = state.overlay.snapshot().unwrap();
        state
            .deliver(
                7,
                "flow",
                parse_document(r#"{"lines":["Newest holder"]}"#).unwrap(),
                SystemTime::now(),
            )
            .unwrap();
        let current = state.overlay.snapshot().unwrap();
        let before = state.document().unwrap();
        let dir = panel_contract::PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let mut control =
            crate::control::PaneControl::open(&dir, state.reader(), Default::default(), || {})
                .unwrap();
        for (incarnation, live) in [(old.incarnation, true), (current.incarnation, false)] {
            let (answer, _) = sync_channel(1);
            control.enqueue_for_tests(Delivery {
                origin: panel_control::PublicationOrigin::Unknown,
                connection: 7,
                what: What::Overlay {
                    panel: parse_document(r#"{"lines":["Must not paint"]}"#).unwrap(),
                    holder: 7,
                    incarnation,
                    authority: Arc::new(Current(live)),
                },
                answer,
            });
        }
        control.apply(&mut state, &mut Default::default(), SystemTime::now());
        assert_eq!(state.document().unwrap(), before);
        assert_eq!(control.did().overlaid, 0);
        assert_eq!(control.did().overlays_refused, 2);
        assert_eq!(state.displayed_source(), Some("flow · hook update refused"));
        control.apply(&mut state, &mut Default::default(), SystemTime::now());
        assert_eq!(control.did().overlays_refused, 2);
    }

    #[test]
    fn overlay_replaces_freshness_and_stamps_actual_arrival() {
        let mut state = held();
        let now = SystemTime::UNIX_EPOCH + std::time::Duration::from_secs(123);
        let hook = parse_document(
            r#"{"updated_at":"producer time","stale_after_minutes":2,"lines":["Fresh"]}"#,
        )
        .unwrap();
        state.apply_overlay(hook, now).unwrap();
        let panel = state.last_good.as_deref().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(panel.written_at, Some(now));
        assert_eq!(panel.updated_at.as_deref(), Some("producer time"));
        assert_eq!(panel.stale_after, std::time::Duration::from_secs(120));
        assert!(!panel.is_stale_at(now + std::time::Duration::from_secs(119)));
        assert!(panel.is_stale_at(now + std::time::Duration::from_secs(121)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn disconnect_keeps_the_document_and_retires_hook_provenance() {
        let mut state = held();
        state
            .apply_overlay(
                parse_document(r#"{"lines":["Hook"]}"#).unwrap(),
                SystemTime::now(),
            )
            .unwrap();
        let before = state.document().unwrap();
        state.release(7);
        assert_eq!(state.document().unwrap(), before);
        assert!(state.displayed_source().is_none());
    }
}

#[cfg(all(test, windows))]
mod native_tests {
    macro_rules! windows_unix_omission {
        ($name:ident) => {
            #[test]
            #[ignore = "unix-only: requires Linux process ancestry, Unix-domain sockets and POSIX PTY fixtures"]
            fn $name() {}
        };
    }

    windows_unix_omission!(native_claude_outside_desktop_path_admits_hook_and_clears_wait);
    windows_unix_omission!(same_client_helper_is_admitted_without_waiting_for_window_apply);
    windows_unix_omission!(nested_client_is_rejected_but_generic_descendant_hook_is_allowed);
    windows_unix_omission!(unheld_and_script_held_refusals_keep_their_contract);
    windows_unix_omission!(
        admitted_proof_survives_helper_exit_but_not_hold_replacement_or_root_death
    );
    windows_unix_omission!(foreign_client_root_and_closed_holder_are_refused);
    windows_unix_omission!(full_admission_queue_refuses_without_changing_the_document);
}
```

```
//! Application-owned provenance is adjacent to the document, never part of producer JSON.
use std::time::SystemTime;

use panel_control::{Connection, PublicationOrigin};

use super::{Panel, PanelState, Row, Style, Theme};

const OUTSIDE: &str = "published from a process outside this console";

/// A failed publication cannot replace the current document's observation.
pub(crate) fn deliver_observed(
    state: &mut PanelState,
    connection: Connection,
    source: &str,
    panel: Panel,
    now: SystemTime,
    origin: PublicationOrigin,
) -> Result<(), String> {
    state.deliver(connection, source, panel, now)?;
    state.published_by = origin;
    Ok(())
}

pub(super) fn append_row(rows: &mut Vec<Row>, state: &PanelState, width: usize, theme: &Theme) {
    if state.published_by == PublicationOrigin::Outside {
        super::wrap(
            rows,
            0,
            OUTSIDE,
            theme.panel_color(None, Some(Style::Warn)),
            width,
        );
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use panel_contract::{PANEL_FILE, PanelDir, parse_document, parse_ops};
    use std::time::Duration;

    fn panel(done: bool) -> Panel {
        parse_document(if done {
            r#"{"header":false,"attention":"done","lines":[{"text":"Body","style":"section","collapsed":true},"content"]}"#
        } else {
            r#"{"header":false,"lines":["content"]}"#
        }).unwrap()
    }

    fn write(dir: &PanelDir, text: &str, at: SystemTime) {
        let path = dir.path().join(PANEL_FILE);
        std::fs::write(&path, text).unwrap();
        std::fs::File::open(path).unwrap().set_modified(at).unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn observation_survives_disconnect_and_only_a_valid_newer_file_replaces_it() {
        let at = SystemTime::UNIX_EPOCH + Duration::from_secs(100);
        let dir = PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let mut state = PanelState::default();
        deliver_observed(
            &mut state,
            1,
            "agent",
            panel(true),
            at,
            PublicationOrigin::Outside,
        )
        .unwrap();
        assert!(!state.document().unwrap().contains(OUTSIDE));
        state.release(1);
        for (text, stamp) in [
            ("{", at + Duration::from_secs(1)),
            (r#"{"lines":["old"]}"#, at),
            (r#"{"lines":["older"]}"#, at - Duration::from_secs(1)),
        ] {
            write(&dir, text, stamp);
            state.refresh(dir.path());
            assert_eq!(state.published_by, PublicationOrigin::Outside);
        }
        write(&dir, r#"{"lines":["new"]}"#, at + Duration::from_secs(2));
        state.refresh(dir.path());
        assert_eq!(state.published_by, PublicationOrigin::Unknown);
    }

    #[test]
    fn patches_keep_observation_refusals_do_not_change_it_and_full_publications_replace_it() {
        let at = SystemTime::UNIX_EPOCH + Duration::from_secs(100);
        let mut state = PanelState::default();
        deliver_observed(
            &mut state,
            1,
            "agent",
            panel(false),
            at,
            PublicationOrigin::Outside,
        )
        .unwrap();
        let ops = parse_ops(&serde_json::json!([{"op":"set","field":"attention","value":"done"}]))
            .unwrap();
        state.patch(1, &ops, at).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(state.published_by, PublicationOrigin::Outside);
        assert!(
            deliver_observed(
                &mut state,
                2,
                "other",
                panel(false),
                at,
                PublicationOrigin::Inside
            )
            .is_err()
        );
        assert_eq!(state.published_by, PublicationOrigin::Outside);
        for origin in [
            PublicationOrigin::Inside,
            PublicationOrigin::Unknown,
            PublicationOrigin::Outside,
        ] {
            deliver_observed(&mut state, 1, "agent", panel(false), at, origin).unwrap();
            assert_eq!(state.published_by, origin);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn app_row_survives_hidden_header_and_block_folds_and_invalidates_cached_layout() {
        let at = SystemTime::now();
        let theme = Theme::default();
        let mut state = PanelState::default();
        deliver_observed(
            &mut state,
            1,
            "agent",
            panel(true),
            at,
            PublicationOrigin::Outside,
        )
        .unwrap();
        let (rows, cached, _) = state
            .lay_out_if_needed(
                "pane",
                super::super::cache::LayoutKey {
                    width: 100,
                    theme,
                    warning: None,
                    chrome: super::super::Chrome::Historical,
                },
                None,
            )
            .unwrap();
        assert!(!cached);
        assert!(rows.iter().any(|row| row.text == OUTSIDE));
        assert!(!rows.iter().any(|row| row.text == "content"));
        assert!(
            state
                .lay_out_if_needed(
                    "pane",
                    super::super::cache::LayoutKey {
                        width: 100,
                        theme,
                        warning: None,
                        chrome: super::super::Chrome::Historical
                    },
                    None
                )
                .unwrap()
                .1
        );
        deliver_observed(
            &mut state,
            1,
            "agent",
            panel(true),
            at,
            PublicationOrigin::Unknown,
        )
        .unwrap();
        let (rows, cached, _) = state
            .lay_out_if_needed(
                "pane",
                super::super::cache::LayoutKey {
                    width: 100,
                    theme,
                    warning: None,
                    chrome: super::super::Chrome::Historical,
                },
                None,
            )
            .unwrap();
        assert!(!cached);
        assert!(!rows.iter().any(|row| row.text == OUTSIDE));
    }

    #[test]
    fn retirement_clears_observation() {
        let dir = PanelDir::create().unwrap();
        let mut state = PanelState::default();
        deliver_observed(
            &mut state,
            1,
            "agent",
            panel(true),
            SystemTime::now(),
            PublicationOrigin::Outside,
        )
        .unwrap();
        state
            .retire(
                1,
                PanelState::retirement_baseline(dir.path()).unwrap(),
                super::super::Retirement::default(),
            )
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(state.published_by, PublicationOrigin::Unknown);
    }

    crate::windows_unix_omission!(
        opened_pane_labels_outside_socket_and_accepts_its_shell_descendant_without_session_capture,
        "unix-only: authenticates a shell descendant through Linux process ancestry and Unix sockets"
    );

    #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
    #[test]
    fn opened_pane_labels_outside_socket_and_accepts_its_shell_descendant_without_session_capture()
    {
        use eframe::egui;
        use std::thread;

        let ctx = egui::Context::default();
        let dir = PanelDir::create().expect("panel directory");
        let endpoint = dir.path().to_owned();
        let id = dir.id().to_owned();
        let mut pane = crate::opening::launch_exact(
            &ctx,
            crate::opening::Launch {
                program: "/bin/sh",
                args: &[],
                directory: None,
                path: None,
            },
            Some(dir),
            0,
            crate::control::ReadActivity::default(),
        )
        .expect("open pane through the application path");
        crate::resume::set_capture(&mut pane, false);
        assert!(pane.restoration.sessions.is_none());

        let outside_dir = endpoint.clone();
        let outside_id = id.clone();
        let outside = thread::spawn(move || {
            panel_control::Producer::of(&outside_dir, &outside_id)
                .expect("outside producer")
                .publish("outside", r#"{"header":false,"lines":["outside body"]}"#)
        });
        drive_pane_until(&mut pane, &ctx, |_| outside.is_finished());
        outside
            .join()
            .expect("outside worker")
            .expect("outside publish accepted");
        let (rows, _, _) = pane
            .panel_state
            .lay_out_if_needed(
                "pane",
                super::super::cache::LayoutKey {
                    width: 100,
                    theme: Theme::default(),
                    warning: None,
                    chrome: super::super::Chrome::Historical,
                },
                None,
            )
            .expect("outside document layout");
        assert!(rows.iter().any(|row| row.text == OUTSIDE));
        assert!(
            pane.panel_state
                .document()
                .unwrap()
                .contains("outside body")
        );
        drive_pane_until(&mut pane, &ctx, |pane| pane.panel_state.held_by().is_none());

        let executable = std::env::current_exe().expect("test executable");
        let executable = executable.to_string_lossy().replace('\'', "'\\''");
        let command = format!(
            "IVY_PROVENANCE_CHILD=1 '{}' --exact panel::provenance_route::tests::publish_from_pane_child --nocapture\n",
            executable
        );
        pane.pty
            .write(command.as_bytes())
            .expect("launch pane descendant");
        drive_pane_until(&mut pane, &ctx, |pane| {
            pane.panel_state
                .last_good
                .as_ref()
                .is_some_and(|panel| panel.lines.iter().any(|line| line.text == "inside"))
        });
        let (rows, _, _) = pane
            .panel_state
            .lay_out_if_needed(
                "pane",
                super::super::cache::LayoutKey {
                    width: 100,
                    theme: Theme::default(),
                    warning: None,
                    chrome: super::super::Chrome::Historical,
                },
                None,
            )
            .expect("inside document layout");
        assert!(!rows.iter().any(|row| row.text == OUTSIDE));
    }

    #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
    fn drive_pane_until(
        pane: &mut crate::Pane,
        ctx: &eframe::egui::Context,
        mut ready: impl FnMut(&crate::Pane) -> bool,
    ) {
        use std::{
            thread,
            time::{Duration, Instant},
        };

        let deadline = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(8);
        while !ready(pane) {
            pane.refresh_its_panel(ctx);
            assert!(
                Instant::now() < deadline,
                "pane did not reach expected state"
            );
            thread::yield_now();
        }
        pane.refresh_its_panel(ctx);
    }
}
```
